[PATCH v6 07/27] vfio/pci: Preserve vfio-pci device files across Live Update
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

Implement the Live Update file handler callbacks to preserve a vfio-pci
device in noiommu mode across a Live Update while keeping it running
across kexec. Subsequent commits will enable userspace to retrieve and
take over this file after the Live Update.

State about each device is serialized into struct vfio_pci_core_device_ser
using Kexec-Handover (KHO) to preserve the pages holding the struct and
the Live Update Orchestrator (LUO) to preserve the physical address of
the struct.

Key design notes and constraints:

 * Noiommu-only policy and bound cdev requirement: can_preserve() verifies
   that the preserved file is a bound vfio-pci cdev file descriptor via
   vfio_file_is_bound_cdev(), and preserve() requires the device to be in
   noiommu mode via vfio_device_is_noiommu() (returning -EOPNOTSUPP
   otherwise). CONFIG_VFIO_PCI_LIVEUPDATE now depends on
   CONFIG_VFIO_DEVICE_CDEV and CONFIG_VFIO_NOIOMMU.

 * Serialized state: During preserve(), serialize the PCI domain, BDF,
   config space size, reset_works flag, and the initial pci_saved_state
   captured when the device was first opened. During freeze(), snapshot
   the virtualized PCI config space (vconfig) and capability map
   (pci_config_map).

 * Keep-running freeze and unfreeze: Do not reset the device, clear
   PCI_COMMAND_MASTER, zap BAR mappings, or revoke dma-bufs during
   freeze(). Validate that VFIO interrupts are disabled, that the device
   is in PCI_D0 (and not in VFIO runtime PM low-power mode), and that
   SR-IOV is not enabled, as VFs are not preserved and the next kernel
   disables SR-IOV on the running device. Then mask physical INTx
   (recording the prior state) and mark the device frozen. If the
   Live Update is aborted after freeze(), unfreeze() restores the physical
   INTx state and clears liveupdate_frozen so the existing FD remains
   usable.

 * TOCTOU synchronization: vfio_pci_dev_is_frozen() is checked locklessly
   on entry to the ioctl, read/write and mmap paths, and re-checked under
   a lock that freeze() holds before any operation that could change the
   device or vconfig: under vdev->igate in vfio_pci_ioctl_set_irqs() and
   around every config-space write in vfio_pci_config_rw_single(), under
   vdev->memory_lock in vfio_pci_runtime_pm_entry(),
   vfio_pci_ioctl_reset() and the SR-IOV enable path of
   vfio_pci_core_sriov_configure(), and under dev_set->lock in
   vfio_pci_dev_set_hot_reset(). A racing operation thus either completes
   before freeze() takes its snapshot or fails, while existing BAR mmaps,
   ioeventfds and dma-bufs keep working. Serializing config writes on
   igate covers the PCI_COMMAND, INTx disable emulation, power state and
   FLR paths at one point (the INTx disable emulation now uses the
   igate-held __vfio_pci_intx_mask() and __vfio_pci_intx_unmask()
   helpers), and also serializes the irq_type and virq_disabled accesses
   of config writes with VFIO_DEVICE_SET_IRQS.

Co-developed-by: David Matlack <dmatlack@google.com>
Signed-off-by: David Matlack <dmatlack@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>
---

[PATCH v6 21/27] vfio: selftests: Add helpers to map DMA memory at
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

Selftest drivers program the IOVA returned by to_iova() into the device.
Because a device in noiommu mode uses those addresses directly as
physical addresses, DMA buffers must be mapped at IOVA == PA so that
to_iova() returns the buffer's physical address without changing the
drivers.

Add __iommu_noiommu_get_pa() to wrap IOMMU_IOAS_NOIOMMU_GET_PA and
iommu_map_identity() to map a physically contiguous buffer at IOVA == PA
by first pinning it at a scratch IOVA (BIT_ULL(52), above the 52-bit
output address space of the noiommu page table), querying its physical
address and contiguity, mapping it at IOVA == PA, and then unmapping the
scratch range while keeping the pages pinned.

Signed-off-by: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>
---

 tools/testing/selftests/vfio/lib/iommu.c      | 61 +++++++++++++++++++

[PATCH v6 06/27] vfio: Add helpers to identify device files that can
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

Add two helpers needed by vfio-pci Live Update support:

1. vfio_file_is_bound_cdev(): checks whether a specific file is a VFIO
   cdev device file that has been bound to an iommufd. Only the bound
   cdev file keeps the device open; the device-level cdev_opened flag
   cannot distinguish between the bound cdev file and a second, unbound
   cdev file opened for the same device.

2. vfio_device_is_noiommu(): move the existing helper out of line and
   export it so vfio-pci can enforce that only devices in no-IOMMU mode
   are preserved across Live Update.

No functional change intended for existing callers.

[PATCH v6 14/27] vfio/pci: Take over preserved devices without
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

When userspace binds a retrieved VFIO cdev file descriptor via
VFIO_DEVICE_BIND_IOMMUFD after kexec, take over the running device in
vfio_pci_core_enable() via vfio_pci_liveupdate_take_over() without
resetting it or clearing bus mastering:

- Skip pci_clear_master(), pci_try_reset_function(), and
  pci_save_state().
- Save PCI_COMMAND before pci_enable_device() and restore the memory and
  I/O decode bits afterwards so pci_enable_device() does not re-enable
  BAR decode (such as I/O port decode) that was disabled before kexec.
- Also restore PCI_COMMAND_INTX_DISABLE, which freeze() set before
  kexec, when vfio-pci uses PCI 2.3 INTx masking for the device
  (vdev->pci_2_3) or masks its broken INTx (vdev->nointx), so INTx stays
  masked until userspace configures interrupts. Otherwise vfio-pci masks
  INTx at the interrupt controller (e.g. with vfio_pci.nointxmask=1), so
  leave DisINTx cleared by pci_enable_device() as a normal open does.
- Build pci_config_map via vfio_config_init(), verify that it matches
  the preserved config map snapshot, and overlay the preserved vconfig
  snapshot onto vdev->vconfig.
- Perform all fallible take-over setup before adopting the preserved
  state. If take-over fails before the device is claimed, leave the
  running device untouched (keeping bus mastering, decode bits, and
  liveupdate_incoming intact) so userspace can retry binding the
  retrieved FD or let the session finish/close reset the unclaimed
  device.
- Once vfio_pci_liveupdate_take_over() succeeds, adopt the preserved
  pci_saved_state and reset_works flag, clear liveupdate_kept_running,
  and free the temporary incoming state; any subsequent error or final
  device close follows the normal vfio_pci_core_disable() path and
  resets the device.

Signed-off-by: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>
---

 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_liveupdate.c |  23 ++++-
 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_priv.h       |  23 ++++-
 3 files changed, 141 insertions(+), 22 deletions(-)

[PATCH v6 13/27] vfio/pci: Protect incoming preserved devices until
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

Protect incoming preserved devices that were kept running across kexec
from being disturbed before userspace either claims them or finishes the
Live Update session:

 * Runtime PM idle D3: In vfio_pci_init_dev(), when
   pci_liveupdate_is_incoming(pdev) is true, latch
   vdev->disable_idle_d3 = true and vdev->liveupdate_kept_running = true.
   Without this, vfio_pci_core_register_device() drops the runtime PM
   reference and allows the PCI runtime PM core to transition an idle
   incoming device into D3hot, stopping bus mastering and potentially
   triggering an internal reset on NoSoftRst- devices.

 * Non-noiommu boot fallback: In vfio_pci_core_register_device(), after
   vfio_register_group_dev() determines whether the device is in noiommu
   mode, if an incoming kept-running device is not in noiommu mode in
   this kernel, log a pci_warn(), clear PCI_COMMAND_MASTER immediately to
   stop untranslated DMA, and record liveupdate_stopped_early so
   finish() resets it without printing a duplicate warning.

 * Bus/slot reset guards: Refuse vfio_pci_dev_set_needs_reset() and
   vfio_pci_dev_set_hot_reset() (-EBUSY) while any device in the dev_set
   has liveupdate_kept_running set, preventing another device in the same
   dev_set from resetting an unclaimed kept-running device. When an
   unclaimed device finishes Live Update and no device in its dev_set is
   open, invoke vfio_pci_dev_set_try_reset() after clearing
   liveupdate_kept_running so devices without function-level reset still
   receive a bus/slot reset.

 * Unbind before finish: In vfio_pci_core_unregister_device(), if a
   still-unclaimed incoming device is unbound from vfio-pci before the
   session finishes (or if a concurrent finish() deferred its function
   reset via liveupdate_reset_pending), clear bus mastering, reset the
   function and restore config state via
   vfio_pci_core_liveupdate_reset_locked() under the device lock already
   held by remove(), and call pci_liveupdate_finish().

Signed-off-by: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>
---

 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_core.c       | 19 +++++++-
 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_liveupdate.c | 16 +++++--
 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_priv.h       | 62 ++++++++++++++++++++++++--
 include/linux/vfio_pci_core.h          |  3 +-

[PATCH v6 11/27] vfio/pci: Retrieve preserved device files after Live Update
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

Enable userspace to retrieve preserved VFIO device files after a Live
Update by implementing the retrieve() and finish() file handler
callbacks.

During retrieve():
 * Validate the handed-over struct vfio_pci_core_device_ser header,
   sizes, and flags.
 * Locate the registered vfio-pci device matching the serialized domain
   and BDF (requiring device->ops == &vfio_pci_ops) under the device
   lock. vfio-pci registers the vfio device before its probe completes,
   and the probe holds that lock, so a device that is still being probed
   is not returned.
 * Require the device to be in noiommu mode in the new kernel (returning
   -EOPNOTSUPP otherwise), still marked incoming by the PCI Live Update
   core, and matching the serialized PCI config space size.
 * Deserialize the inherited struct pci_saved_state and copy the
   handed-over vconfig and pci_config_map into a kernel-owned struct
   vfio_pci_liveupdate_incoming attached to vdev under dev_set->lock via
   vfio_pci_liveupdate_attach_incoming(), leaving the KHO serialized
   buffer read-only.
 * Open and return an anonymous VFIO cdev file via
   vfio_device_liveupdate_cdev_open().

Do not implement can_finish(): closing a retrieved LUO session invokes
finish, and refusing can_finish() would leak the session and leave
unclaimed devices running. Instead, finish() always completes:
 * If the retrieved device was claimed by opening it via
   VFIO_DEVICE_BIND_IOMMUFD (which consumes vdev->liveupdate_incoming;
   subsequent patches replace the open-time reset with non-reset
   take-over, reject opening incoming devices other than via retrieve(),
   and protect incoming kept-running devices across runtime PM, dev_set
   resets, and non-noiommu boots), finish() simply completes its PCI
   Live Update participation.
 * If the device was not claimed (never retrieved, retrieve() failed, or
   retrieved but never bound via VFIO_DEVICE_BIND_IOMMUFD),
   vfio_pci_core_liveupdate_reset() logs one pci_warn() naming the
   device, clears PCI_COMMAND_MASTER, resets the function, and restores
   the inherited pci_saved_state just as closing the device would. If
   the device has a function reset but is not reset, e.g. because
   pci_dev_trylock() contends with a concurrent driver unbind, latch
   vdev->liveupdate_reset_pending so vfio_pci_core_unregister_device()
   completes the function reset and state restoration via
   vfio_pci_core_liveupdate_reset_locked() under the device lock held by
   device_release_driver(). Opening the device after finish() resets it
   like any open, so it drops the latch.
 * If the device was not retrieved, look it up under pci_dev_lock(),
   which waits for a vfio-pci probe in progress. If it is bound to
   vfio-pci, finish it as above. If it has no driver, clear bus
   mastering and reset it. Otherwise leave it to the bound driver.
 * Call pci_liveupdate_finish() and kho_restore_free() on every path.

Co-developed-by: David Matlack <dmatlack@google.com>
Signed-off-by: David Matlack <dmatlack@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>
---
 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_core.c       |  65 +++++
 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_liveupdate.c | 342 ++++++++++++++++++++++++-
 drivers/vfio/pci/vfio_pci_priv.h       |  75 ++++++
 include/linux/vfio_pci_core.h          |   6 +
 4 files changed, 484 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

[PATCH v6 08/27] vfio: Introduce vfio_find_device() helper
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

Introduce vfio_find_device() to discover a registered vfio_device from
vfio_device_class matching user-provided criteria.

To ensure safe lifecycle handling, vfio_find_device() acquires a
registration reference (device->refcount) via
vfio_device_try_get_registration() rather than relying solely on
device.kref taken in class_find_device(). Holding only kref allows
vendor modules to unload while external callers hold device pointers,
leading to a kernel panic on release. Callers must release the device
via vfio_device_put_registration().

Additionally, move refcount_set() prior to vfio_device_add() in
__vfio_register_dev() so device->refcount is initialized before the
device is published to class lookups, avoiding registration data races.

On vfio_device_add() failure, the error path drops the initial
registration reference and waits for completion to drain any reference
taken by a concurrent lookup before returning the error to the driver.

Signed-off-by: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>
---
 drivers/vfio/vfio_main.c | 64 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++---
 include/linux/vfio.h     |  2 ++
 2 files changed, 62 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

[PATCH v6 01/27] vfio/pci: Factor out the reset logic in VFIO PCI
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

vfio_pci_core_disable() is a very long function doing multiple things
like exiting runtime power management, setting device to D0 power,
clearing bus master, clearing irqs, freeing eventfds, releasing device
regions, unmapping PCI bars, and performing device/bus resets.

reset logic.

vfio_pci_core_try_reset(), and update vfio_pci_core_disable() to use

[PATCH v6 05/27] PCI: liveupdate: Add helpers to preserve struct pci_saved_state
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

Drivers that keep a device running across Live Update need to hand the
state they captured with pci_store_saved_state() to the next kernel,
since the next kernel never sees the device in that state. VFIO, for
example, captures it at first open and uses it to restore the device
when the last user closes it.

struct pci_saved_state is private to the PCI core and its in-memory
layout is not a stable ABI. Add helpers to serialize it into a
caller-provided buffer and rebuild it in the next kernel with a stable
struct pci_saved_state_ser ABI. Using caller-provided buffers allows
drivers to embed the serialized saved state directly in their own
KHO-preserved data with a single allocation and lifetime.

Validate the serialized buffer and verify that the saved standard
configuration header matches the live device before rebuilding the
state: the vendor and device IDs must match, only Type 0 (Normal)
headers are accepted, and BAR0-5 and the expansion ROM BAR address must
equal the current hardware values. This ensures that restoring the
saved state cannot write stale BAR addresses if resources were
reassigned in the new kernel. Only the address bits of the ROM BAR are
compared, since its enable and validation status bits may legitimately
differ.

Signed-off-by: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>
---
 Documentation/PCI/liveupdate.rst |   3 +
 drivers/pci/liveupdate.c         | 223 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 drivers/pci/pci.c                |   5 -
 drivers/pci/pci.h                |   6 +
 include/linux/kho/abi/pci.h      |  47 +++++++
 include/linux/pci_liveupdate.h   |   7 +
 6 files changed, 286 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

[PATCH v6 00/27] VFIO PCI Live Update (noiommu mode)
Author: Vipin Sharma <vipinsh@google.com>

This series adds a fully working Live Update support for vfio-pci
devices in noiommu mode, using the Live Update Orchestrator (LUO) and
Kexec HandOver (KHO).

This series can also be found on GitHub:
  https://github.com/shvipin/linux/tree/vfio/liveupdate/v6

Unlike v5, which reset the device in freeze() and required userspace to
re-initialize it after kexec, v6 keeps a preserved device running across
the kexec: it is not reset, bus mastering and memory decode stay enabled,
it stays in D0, and in-flight DMA continues into preserved memory (e.g. a
LUO-preserved shmem memfd). In the new kernel, userspace retrieves the
device FD from its LUO session and takes over the running device without
resetting it on the first VFIO_DEVICE_BIND_IOMMUFD.

Preserving IOMMU-backed devices is refused with -EOPNOTSUPP until LUO can
preserve IOMMU state; group/container FDs and unbound cdev FDs are refused
with -ENOENT.

This series is built on top of PCI Live Update series version v10:
  https://lore.kernel.org/linux-pci/20261013162529.1928093-1-dmatlack@google.com/

Design
======

- Preserve: only a bound noiommu vfio-pci cdev FD can be preserved (no
  VFs, Intel display devices, or zdev). The device is registered with PCI
  Live Update and its pre-open struct pci_saved_state is serialized into
  a versioned KHO buffer ("vfio-pci-v1").

- Freeze/unfreeze: freeze() requires interrupts to be disabled, the
  device to be in D0, and SR-IOV to be disabled (VFs are not preserved).
  It snapshots vconfig and pci_config_map, and masks physical INTx while
  leaving bus mastering enabled. If the kexec is aborted, unfreeze()
  restores INTx and the existing FD keeps working.

- Early protection in the new kernel: an incoming device is kept out of
  runtime D3, sibling dev_set resets are blocked, and opening it via its
  cdev or VFIO_GROUP_GET_DEVICE_FD fails with -EBUSY until the session is
  finished. If the new kernel is not in noiommu mode, bus mastering is
  cleared at registration.

- Retrieve and take-over: LIVEUPDATE_SESSION_RETRIEVE_FD validates the
  serialized state against the hardware and returns an unbound cdev FD.
  The first VFIO_DEVICE_BIND_IOMMUFD takes over the device without
  clearing bus mastering, resetting, or re-saving its state, restores the
  preserved vconfig, and keeps the original saved state so that normal
  reset/restore applies when the FD is closed.

- Unclaimed devices: if the session is finished or closed without taking
  over the device (not retrieved, retrieved but not bound, unbound, or
  driverless), the kernel logs one pci_warn(), resets the device, and
  restores its pre-Live-Update state.

- DMA memory ordering: a running device keeps DMAing until it is taken
  over or reset, so its DMA memory must stay pinned until then. LUO
  finishes files in LIFO order, so userspace must preserve the DMA memory
  (e.g. a memfd) in the same session and before the device. This is
  documented in Documentation/driver-api/vfio_pci_liveupdate.rst.

Testing
=======

- QEMU with an emulated igb device in noiommu mode, across real
  kexec reboots: vfio_pci_liveupdate_uapi_test and
  vfio_pci_liveupdate_kexec_test (opens fail with -EBUSY, memfd stays at
  the same physical addresses, pre-kexec DMA completes after take-over,
  and new DMA works without re-init).
- Physical Intel hosts with DSA (8086:0b25), across real kexec
  reboots: vfio_pci_liveupdate_kexec_test with DMA started just before
  the kexec (opens fail with -EBUSY, memfd stays at the same physical
  addresses, pre-kexec DMA completes correctly after take-over, and new
  DMA works without re-init).


  $ ./setup.sh 0000:6a:01.0
  $ ./vfio_pci_liveupdate_kexec_test -s 1 0000:6a:01.0
  $ kexec --reuse-cmdline -l -s /boot/vmlinuz
  $ kexec -e
  $ ./setup.sh 0000:6a:01.0
  $ ./vfio_pci_liveupdate_kexec_test -s 2 0000:6a:01.0

AI disclosure
=============

New and reworked patches were developed with LLM assistance. Assisted-by
is not needed now in linux kernel but I thought I should at least
disclose this in cover letter.

v6:

- Keep noiommu devices running across kexec and take them over without
  reset, instead of resetting them in freeze().
- Restrict preservation to bound noiommu cdev FDs.
- Add PCI helpers to serialize and restore struct pci_saved_state.
- Add unfreeze() and close races between freeze() and ioctl/config
  access. Refuse freeze() while SR-IOV is enabled, and refuse enabling
  SR-IOV on a frozen device.
- Protect incoming devices in the new kernel until they finish Live
  Update; reset unclaimed devices in finish() with a single pci_warn().
  retrieve() and finish() look the device up under the device lock, so
  they never act on a device that vfio-pci is still probing.
- Fix vfio_pci_liveupdate_init() error handling to avoid a double
  unregister at module exit (reported by Sashiko on v5).
- Drop "vfio/pci: Export vfio_pci_dma_buf_move for vfio-pci module",
  "vfio: selftests: Expose iommu_modes to tests", and exports that were
  only needed by the v5 reset path.
- Update the VFIO PCI and PCI Live Update documentation.
- Selftests: add an iommufd noiommu mode, IOVA == PA mapping helpers,
  2MB THP memfd helpers, an attach() driver op (igb, DSA), and a kexec
  test with DMA running across the Live Update.

[PATCH v11 11/14] s390/vfio-ap: Callback to get the size of data to be migrated during guest migration
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

Callback to get the estimated data length of the data that will be required

VFIO_DEVICE_FEATURE_MIGRATION migration support.

which is stored with the VFIO device when the 'vfio_device'

[PATCH v11 08/14] s390/vfio-ap: Transition device migration state from STOP to RESUMING
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

Transitioning from VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP to VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_RESUMING
starts a process of restoring the device state of the vfio device on the
target system. To prepare for restoring the device state, open a file
stream to receive the guest's AP configuration saved when the device state
on the source system transitioned from VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP to
VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP_COPY.

Note:
This commit introduces the file operations structure for migration state
files with a stub implementation of the write operation. The complete
implementation is provided in the subsequent commit.

This incremental approach allows for easier review of the migration
infrastructure setup separate from the actual implementation logic.

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>
Reviewed-by: Jason J. Herne <jjherne@linux.ibm.com>

[PATCH v11 10/14] s390/vfio-ap: Transition device migration state to from/to STOP
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_RESUMING to VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP
VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP_COPY to VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Terminates the data transfer session of the vfio-ap device state
between the source and target hosts. Release the migration file
state (ap_config buffers and file pointers) so that a subsequent
migration captures a fresh AP configuration rather than serving
stale data. Since the vfio-ap device does not virtualize a DMA
device, there is no internal device state to incorporate into the
vfio-ap device on the target, so return NULL since no file stream
is needed in the STOP state.

VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP to VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_RUNNING
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Migration has been cancelled. Release the migration file state so
that a subsequent migration captures a fresh AP configuration
rather than serving stale data.

VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_RUNNING to VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Migration has not yet been initiated. No migration file state
exists to release, so just return NULL.

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

[PATCH v11 12/14] s390/vfio-ap: Set up for vfio-ap device migration
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

In order to facilitate vfio-ap device migration:

* The function pointers to the callbacks used to facilitate migration of a
  vfio-ap device must be plugged in. These function pointers are specified
  via the fields contained in a struct vfio_migration_ops instance:

  	migration_set_state: Set the migration state of the vfio-ap device
  	migration_get_state: Get the migration state of the vfio-ap device
  	migration_get_data_size: Get the estimated data number bytes of
  				 data that will be required to complete
  				 STOP_COPY
* The migration_flags field of the struct vfio_device instance is set to
  VFIO_MIGRATION_STOP_COPY to indicate that STOP, STOP_COPY and RESUMING
  are the migration states supported for vfio-ap device migration.

* The initial migration state of the vfio-ap device is set to RUNNING

* The boolean flag indicating that all data sent from userspace during the
  RESUMINMG phase has been received is initialized to false.

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

[PATCH v11 03/14] s390/vfio-ap: Functions to initialize/release vfio device migration data
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

Provides the functions that initialize and release the data structures
used during live guest migration:

* vfio_ap_init_migration_data

  Allocates and initializes the object used to maintain the state of the
  VFIO migration. It is called when the VFIO device is opened.

* vfio_ap_release_migration_data

  Frees the memory of the object used to maintain the VFIO migration
  state. It is called when the VFIO device release callback is invoked
  and when the VFIO device is closed.

* vfio_ap_release_mig_files

  This function is called from the vfio_ap_release_migration_data
  function (above) and releases the vfio_ap_migration_file objects
  contained within the vfio_ap_migation_data object used to maintain
  the state of the VFIO migration process.

* vfio_ap_release_stop_copy_file

  This function is called from the vfio_ap_release_mig_files function
  (above) to release the vfio_ap_migration_file used during the
  STOP_COPY phase of migration. For now, this is a stub function that
  will be fully implemented in a subsequent patch after the
  vfio_ap_config object referenced within is allocated, as it will
  need to be freed according to how it is allocated.

* vfio_ap_release_resuming_file

  This function is called from the vfio_ap_release_mig_files function
  (above) to release the vfio_ap_migration_file used during the
  RESUMING phase of migration. For now, this is a stub function that
  will be fully implemented in a subsequent patch after the
  vfio_ap_config object referenced within is allocated, as it will
  need to be freed according to how it is allocated.

* vfio_ap_set_state, vfio_ap_get_state and vfio_ap_get_data_size

  These three functions are callback functions assigned to the
  vfio_ap_migration_ops (.migration_set_state, .migration_get_state
  and .migration_get_data_size function pointers).

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

[PATCH v11 00/14] s390/vfio-ap: Add live guest migration support
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

This patch series implements live guest migration support for KVM guests
with s390 AP (Adjunct Processor) devices passed through via the VFIO
mediated device framework.

Background
~~~~~~~~~~

The vfio-ap device driver differs from typical VFIO device drivers in that
it does not virtualize a physical device. Instead, it manages AP
configuration metadata identifying the AP adapters, domains, and controls390/vfio-ap: Add live guest migration support
domains to which a guest will be granted access. These AP resources are
configured by assigning them to a vfio-ap mediated device via its sysfs
assignment interfaces. When the fd for the VFIO device is opened by
userspace, the vfio_ap device driver sets the guest's AP configuration
from the metadata stored with the mediated device. As such, the AP devices
are not accessed directly through the vfio_ap driver, so the driver has no
internal AP device state to migrate. What it does migrate is the AP
configuration metadata of the source guest.

Implementation Approach
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

This series implements the VFIO migration protocol using the STOP_COPY
migration flow. The key aspects are:

1. On transition of the migration state from STOP to STOP_COPY
   - The vfio_ap device driver creates a filestream for userspace to use to
     read the guest's AP configuration from the mdev

2. During the STOP_COPY phase
   - Userspace uses the filestream created in #1 to read the source guest's
     AP configuration
   - The vfio_ap device driver copies the source guest's AP configuration
     information to userspace

3. On transition of the migration state from STOP to RESUMING
   - The vfio_ap device driver creates a filestream for userspace to use to
     write the source guest's AP configuration information so it can be
     restored to the mdev on the destination host.

4. During the RESUMING phase
   - Userspace uses the filestream created in #3 to send the source guest's
     AP configuration information to the vfio_ap device driver on the
     destination host.
   - The vfio_ap device driver first verifies the source guest's AP
     configuration is compatible with the destination host's.
   - The driver restores AP configuration to the mdev on the destination
     host which automatically hot plugs the AP resources identified
     therein.

5. Documentation
   - Add live guest migration chapter to vfio-ap.rst

Compatibility Validation
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

The series includes comprehensive validation to ensure source and
destination AP configurations are compatible. For each queue, the following
characteristics must match:

- AP type (target must be same or newer than source)
- Installed facilities (APSC, APQKM, AP4KC, SLCF)
- Operating mode (CCA, Accelerator, XCP)
- APXA facility setting
- Classification (native vs stateless functions)
- Queue usability (binding/associated state)

When incompatibilities are detected, migration fails with detailed error
messages identifying the specific queue and characteristic that caused
the failure.

Configuration Management
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

This implementation does not prevent configuration changes during
migration. Configuration stability is an orchestration-layer
responsibility, consistent with other VFIO device types. The driver's
role is to validate configurations and provide clear diagnostics when
incompatibilities are detected, enabling orchestration tools to implement
appropriate policies.

QEMU patches exploiting this series:
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
https://lore.kernel.org/qemu-devel/20260409141352.997844-1-akrowiak@linux.ibm.com/

[PATCH v11 05/14] s390/vfio-ap: Callback to get/set vfio device mig state during guest migration
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

Implements two callbacks related to the current state of a vfio-ap device:
* The callback that sets a new migration state of a vfio-ap device during
  live migration of guests with pass-through access to AP devices. This
  callback is mandatory for VFIO_DEVICE_FEATURE_MIGRATION support.

  The function pointer for this callback is specified via the
  'migration_set_state' field of the 'vfio_migration_ops' structure
  which is stored with the VFIO device when the 'vfio_device'
  structure representing the mediated device is initialized.

* The callback that returns the current vfio device migration state during
  live migration of guests with pass-through access to AP devices.

  The function pointer for this callback is specified via the
  'migration_get_state' field of the 'vfio_migration_ops' structure
  which is stored with the VFIO device when the 'vfio_device'
  structure representing the mediated device is initialized.

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

[PATCH v11 09/14] s390/vfio-ap: File ops called to resume the vfio device migration
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

Implements the 'write' callback function for the file stream created to
restore the state of the vfio-ap device on the destination host during the
RESUMING phase.

Userspace reads the source guest's saved AP configuration metadata from the
STOP_COPY stream and writes it to this stream in chunks. The write callback
accumulates the stream into a pre-allocated vfio_ap_config buffer. Once all
data has arrived, the configuration is validated against the destination
mdev's pre-configured shadow_apcb and hardware capabilities.

In order for the source guest's configuration to be considered compatible:
* The migration data must carry the expected magic number and version.
* Every APQN and control domain in the source configuration must be present
  in the destination mdev's shadow_apcb.
* The hardware facilities and AP type of each queue must be compatible
  between source and destination.

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>
---
 drivers/s390/crypto/vfio_ap_migration.c | 600 +++++++++++++++++++++++-
 1 file changed, 598 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

[PATCH v11 04/14] s390/vfio-ap: Reset migration state in VFIO_DEVICE_RESET ioctl handler
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

According to the VFIO migration specifications, userspace must issue a
VFIO_DEVICE_RESET ioctl if a vfio device enters an error state or fails a
state transition to recover the device and return its migration state to
RUNNING. This patch adds a function that is called when a VFIO_DEVICE_RESET
is issued that releases any open migration file buffers and resets the
migration state to RUNNING.

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

[PATCH v11 07/14] s390/vfio-ap: File ops called to save the vfio device migration state
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

Implements the read callback function that was added to the
file_operations structure for the file created to save the state of the
vfio-ap device when the migration state transitioned from STOP to
to the STOP_COPY state.

This function copies the guest's AP configuration information to
userspace. The information copied is comprised of the APQN of each queue
device passed through to the guest along with its hardware information.
This state data will be transferred to the vfio_ap device driver on the
destination host when the state is transitioned to RESUMING.

[PATCH v11 06/14] s390/vfio-ap: Transition guest migration state from STOP to STOP_COPY
Author: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

The transition of the guest migration state from VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP to
VFIO_DEVICE_STATE_STOP_COPY begins the process of saving the
vfio device state. The vfio device state is comprised of the guest's
AP configuration which specifies the adapters, domains and control
domains to which the guest will be given access.

To begin the process of saving the vfio device state, the VFIO AP device
driver opens a file stream that will be used to read the source guest's
AP configuration.

Note:
This commit introduces the file operations structure for migration state
files with a stub implementation of the read operation. The complete
implementation is provided in the subsequent commit.

This incremental approach allows for easier review of the migration
infrastructure setup separate from the actual implementation logic.

Signed-off-by: Anthony Krowiak <akrowiak@linux.ibm.com>

[PATCH 11/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Add Realm stage-2 MMU operations
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


Register the Realm stage-2 MMU operations now that the RMM mapping
and unmapping helpers are available. Route unmapping through the
RMM, and use the existing fault handler for Realm memory faults.

The RMM handles TLB invalidation when stage-2 mappings change, so
the host has no separate TLB flush to perform. Realm stage-2 does
not support access flags, including for shared IPAs.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

[PATCH 09/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Prevent memslot modifications after Realm creation
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


Once the Realm is created, prevent any changes to guest_memfd backed
memslots, as the private memory is provided by slots backed by gmem.
This will also be validated when we handle a fault for Realm Stage2 (in
a later commit).

Additionally, similar to pKVM, we do not support dirty logging or
read-only memslots.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

[PATCH 10/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Support runtime faulting of memory
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

At runtime if the realm guest accesses memory which hasn't yet been
mapped then KVM needs to either populate the region or fault the guest.

For memory in the lower (protected) region of IPA, verify that the
corresponding guest_memfd page is private before delegating to the RMM.
The RMM then maps the page into the realm and initializes its contents
to zero.

For memory in the upper (shared) IPA region, map the backing page as
non-secure memory. Check that a guest_memfd page is shared when the
fault uses guest_memfd; ordinary userspace-backed memslots follow the
normal user memory fault path.

If the fault IPA and guest_memfd attributes disagree then exit to
userspace with KVM_EXIT_MEMORY_FAULT so the VMM can perform the required
conversion.

Co-developed-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>
---

[PATCH 07/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Tear down RTTs
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

The RMM owns the stage 2 page tables for a realm, and KVM must request
that the RMM creates/destroys entries as necessary. The physical pages
to store the page tables are delegated to the realm as required, and can
be undelegated when no longer used.

Creating new RTTs is the easy part, tearing down is a little more
tricky. The result of realm_rtt_destroy() can be used to effectively
walk the tree and destroy the entries (undelegating pages that were
given to the realm).

Unmap the Realm IPA space once during stage-2 teardown. Defer destruction
of non-root RTTs until final Realm destruction, after REALM_TERMINATE and
before destroying the RD; root RTTs remain until the RD has gone away.

Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>
---

 arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c             |  17 ++-
 arch/arm64/kvm/rmi.c             | 189 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 3 files changed, 214 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

[PATCH 02/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Introduce Realms
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


Add foundational work for supporting Realms.
 - Add a new VM flavor.
 - At KVM init, check if the KVM can support Realms (though not functional
   yet) and will be advertised by static key kvm_rmi_is_available. This
   will be turned on in a later patch, once we have all the bits and
   pieces ready. For now check if we are blessed with KVM_MODE_RMM.
 - Add realm specific tracking in kvm_arch. Since Realm and protected pKVM
   states are mutually exclusive, move them into a union.
 - Also adjust the memslot preparation checks to reflect the changes.

Please note that we cannot create Realm VMs yet. This requires further
changes to the UABI and core RMI driver support, which will come later.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

[PATCH 00/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Prepare Realm memory management
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

This series adds the memory plumbing for CCA building on the basic
plumbing[1] that has just been merged.

These patches add the internal realm VM state and RMM feature checks,
prepare realm stage-2 setup and teardown, and provide RTT mapping and
invalidation helpers. Realm memslots are now contrained to the lower
half of the IPA space, because faults treat the protected and shared
halves as aliasing. The final patch then connects these helpers to the
KVM stage-2 MMU operations.

There's also an integration branch[2] that builds on this series and
includes the realm descriptor creation, REC entry/exit, activation and
the new uAPI. This series by itself is also available as a git
branch[3]. However the new code isn't generally active without the full
integration branch.

[1] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261007073537.2454351-1-suzuki.poulose@arm.com/
[2] https://gitlab.arm.com/linux-arm/linux-cca
    branch: cca-host-mm/v1/integration
[3] https://gitlab.arm.com/linux-arm/linux-cca
    branch: cca-host-mm/v1/mm

[PATCH v3 6/8] KVM: SEV: Add SNP vCPU state get and set commands
Author: Jörg Rödel <joro@8bytes.org>


Add KVM_SEV_SNP_GET_VCPU_STATE and KVM_SEV_SNP_SET_VCPU_STATE as
vCPU-scoped KVM_MEMORY_ENCRYPT_OP commands and advertise
KVM_CAP_SNP_VCPU_STATE.  The shared, extensible argument reports the VMSA
and GHCB GPAs and has validity bits for both addresses.

For SET, validate the argument, launch ordering, VMSA alignment, and that a
valid VMSA is backed by guest_memfd.  Install or invalidate the VMSA and
GHCB as requested.  GET returns the currently tracked guest-owned VMSA GPA
and the GHCB address.

Require KVM_CAP_SNP_DIRECT_VMSA for SET.  The VM-scoped capability fixes
the launch model before vCPU creation; the per-vCPU command only selects
the guest-owned VMSA and GHCB addresses for that vCPU.

Route the commands through the locked vCPU ioctl hook.  The vCPU mutex
serializes the commands against launch finalization, which locks all vCPUs
before inspecting their state.

Signed-off-by: Joerg Roedel <joerg.roedel@amd.com>

[PATCH v3 0/8] KVM: SVM: Support direct setting of VMSA for SEV-SNP guests
Author: Jörg Rödel <joro@8bytes.org>


Hi,

here is the updated version of KVM-AMD changes to allow user-space to
directly set the boot VMSA (direct-vmsa) for an SEV-SNP virtual
machine.

The direct-vmsa support is required for stable launch measurements
when loading the initial SEV-SNP guest image from an IGVM file.
Without this support KVM measures a VMSA at a fixed GPA for every
VCPU, which makes the launch measurement dependent on the number of
VCPUs. Besides that the current KVM VMSA-allocation behavior poses
challenges for live migration of SEV-SNP VMs. The current behavior is
fully preserved when direct-vmsa is not used.

For a longer justification have a look at this email from the v1
thread:
	
	https://lore.kernel.org/kvm/aiqwjn2DVOumfr3T@8bytes.org/

The user-space API remains unchanged since v2 of the series and
consists of three parts:

	- A new KVM_CAP_SNP_DIRECT_VMSA VM-level capability which can
	  be queried and enabled from user-space. When user-space
	  enabled the capability, KVM will no longer allocate VMSA
	  pages when creating VCPUs. This means that the capability
	  can only be enabled before any VCPUs are created.

	- Allow VMSA pages in SNP launch updates. This was already
	  present in the previous version, but has different semantics
	  now. In this version all it does is adding the VMSA pages to
	  the initial image and the launch measurement. 
	
	- A new per-VCPU MEM_ENC operation to get and set the VMSA and
	  GHCB addresses of a VCPU. User-space will use this operation
	  to set the GPA of the launch VMSA of the VCPUs, but can
	  also query and set the addresses during runtime. 

	  This operation is designed to support the requirements of
	  future live-migration support, which needs to be able to set
	  and get VMSA as well as GHCB addresses. Since this
	  functionality is a superset of the operation needed for
	  direct-vmsa support, it goes the extra mile and allows
	  import and export of all SEV-SNP specific VCPU state.

The changes are rebased to v7.4-rc1 and tested with lockdep enabled.

Please review.

-Joerg

Joerg Roedel (8):
  KVM: SEV: Document SNP direct VMSA userspace ABI
  KVM: SVM: Implement GET_AP_APIC_IDS NAE event
  KVM: SVM: Hold SRCU while reloading guest-owned VMSAs
  KVM: SEV: Add direct VMSA capability
  KVM: SEV: Allow VMSA pages in SNP launch updates
  KVM: SEV: Add SNP vCPU state get and set commands
  KVM: selftests: Test the SNP APIC-ID-list GHCB request
  KVM: selftests: Test SNP vCPU state and direct VMSA launch

[PATCH v3 5/8] KVM: SEV: Allow VMSA pages in SNP launch updates
Author: Jörg Rödel <joro@8bytes.org>


Pass the firmware VMSA page type through KVM_SEV_SNP_LAUNCH_UPDATE.
Only accept VMSA pages when userspace has enabled
KVM_CAP_SNP_DIRECT_VMSA.
Require each request to describe exactly one 4-KiB VMSA page.  Allow
repeated requests and keep VMSA creation independent of association with a
vCPU.

The VMSA's VMPL and SEV features define its execution context.  They must
agree with KVM's VM-wide configuration.  Before passing a VMSA to firmware,
require VMPL 0.  Require sev_features to exactly match the VM's configured
VMSA features.  Treat the remaining contents as guest-owned data.

Signed-off-by: Joerg Roedel <joerg.roedel@amd.com>

[PATCH v3 4/8] KVM: SEV: Add direct VMSA capability
Author: Jörg Rödel <joro@8bytes.org>


Direct VMSA mode is currently selected implicitly when userspace installs
a guest-owned VMSA on any vCPU.  That makes the launch model a side effect
of per-vCPU state and applies it retroactively to every vCPU.

Add KVM_CAP_SNP_DIRECT_VMSA as a VM-scoped settable capability.  Require
userspace to enable it on an SNP VM before creating vCPUs, and reject
nonzero arguments.

When enabled, skip allocating KVM-owned VMSAs during vCPU creation and
skip generating and measuring KVM-owned VMSAs at launch finish.  Keep
legacy allocation and measurement behavior when it is not enabled.

Signed-off-by: Joerg Roedel <joerg.roedel@amd.com>
---

[PATCH v3 3/8] KVM: SVM: Hold SRCU while reloading guest-owned VMSAs
Author: Jörg Rödel <joro@8bytes.org>


Take a dedicated KVM SRCU read lock around the guest-owned VMSA memslot
and guest_memfd lookup.  This makes the reload path self-contained and
prevents it from racing a userspace memslot replacement.

Keep the lock through VMCB installation, and release it on all lookup
failure paths.

Signed-off-by: Joerg Roedel <joerg.roedel@amd.com>

[PATCH RESEND v2 20/21] mm/pgtable: disallow calling folded
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>


pXdp_get() could return a dummy value for a page-table level folded at
compile time. Passing this value to set_pXd() could cause unexpected
behavior.

Prevent this at compile time by ensuring that all calls to
set_pgd()/set_p4d()/set_pud() are compiled out for folded page-table
levels. Make the compiler complain if these helpers are called with
dummy values.

Since there are places where set_pXd() is called even when
the corresponding page-table level is folded at compile time,
drop the incorrect comments for set_pXd().

Signed-off-by: David Hildenbrand (Arm) <david@kernel.org>
Co-developed-by: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>
---

[PATCH RESEND v2 19/21] mm/pgtable: disallow calling
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>


These helpers silently fallback to the folded helpers. With e.g., pdgp_get()
returning dummy values, pgd_page_vaddr(pgdp_get(pgdp)) will now behave
differently than pgd_page_vaddr(*pgdp).

Disallow calling (pgd|p4d|pud)_page() entirely, and catch when passing a
dummy value to pgd_page_vaddr() and (p4d|pud)_pgtable.
This will catch most abuse of dummy pXdp value at compile time --
so far there were none, and it better stay that way.

Signed-off-by: David Hildenbrand (Arm) <david@kernel.org>
Co-developed-by: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>

[PATCH RESEND v2 18/21] mm/pgtable: catch abuse of folded dummy
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>


Catch and prevent all abuse with dummy values on the stack
similar to:

	pud_t pud = pudp_get(pudp);
	pmd_t *pmdp = pud_offset(&pud, addr);

While this approach relies on the compiler propagating constants, it
should catch most of the issues in practice. It would have caught all
the issues were found through manual inspection.

To avoid build issues particularly on x86, where pgd_val() might not be
around in some inclusion paths, perform the new checks from wrapper
macros.

[PATCH RESEND v2 17/21] mm/pgtable: optimize pmdp_get() and
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>


Using pmdp_get() and friends in common code on a kernel config with
folded page tables is suboptimal: they default to a READ_ONCE(), forcing
the compiler to actually read that value even though it will not actually
be used afterwards.

This was recently reported by Christophe Leroy [1] and block conversion
of more common code to pmdp_get() and friends.

(using pgdp_get() as one example)

Most of the code ignores the result from pgdp_get() on configs with
folded page tables entirely, as it's hardcoded:

        pgd_present()==1 && pgd_leaf()==false

Common code will just treat it as a "this is a page table" and call
p4d_offset() or p4d_offset_lockless() for the next lower level, where
the obtained pgdp_get() result is ignored entirely.

So, return a dummy value and avoid any memory reads.

There is a catch, though:

1) If code calls pgd_val() and somehow relies on the data, it would now
   see dummy values. The code really must be aware of folded page table
   levels. Fortunately, code usually ignores pgd_val() completely for
   page tables (with ptdump being one exception when calculating
   effective permissions). it's checked + fixed the x86 ptdump mechanism.

2) If code passes the pgd_t to a function that would work on the result,
   it would now see dummy values. The only concern is really passing
   the pgd_t on the stack as a pointer to p4d_offset(). Most code that
   would do that, should actually use p4d_offset_lockless(), which
   handles this properly. it's checked + fixed problematic instances.

3) Calling set_pgd() / pgd_page() / pgd_page_vaddr() with a pgd_t obtained through
   pgdp_get(). There was once such case in riscv code with set_pgd(), which
   is fixed.

As an example, this is the generated code for perf_get_page_size() with
PGTABLE_LEVELS=3 on arm64:

Before:
00000000000052a0 <perf_get_page_size>:
    ...
    52dc: d53b4234      mrs     x20, DAIF
    52e0: d50343df      msr     DAIFSet, #0x3
    ...
    52fc: d35e9a69      ubfx    x9, x19, #30, #9        /* pud_offset_lockless() */
    5300: f9403508      ldr     x8, [x8, #0x68]
    5304: f869790a      ldr     x10, [x8, x9, lsl #3]   /* pudp_get() */
    5308: f90007ea      str     x10, [sp, #0x8]
    530c: f8697908      ldr     x8, [x8, x9, lsl #3]    /* pudp_get() */
    ...
    5360: 90000009      adrp    x9, 0x5000 <perf_prepare_sample+0x548>
    5364: 92746908      and     x8, x8, #0x7ffffff000
    5368: d3557675      ubfx    x21, x19, #21, #9       /* pmd_offset_lockless() */
    ...
    5394: f8757ac8      ldr     x8, [x22, x21, lsl #3]  /* pmdp_get() */

After:
0000000000052a0 <perf_get_page_size>:
    ...
    52dc: d53b4234      mrs     x20, DAIF
    52e0: d50343df      msr     DAIFSet, #0x3
    ...                                                 /* no pud_offset_lockless() and pudp_get() */
    5318: 90000009      adrp    x9, 0x5000 <perf_prepare_sample+0x548>
    531c: 92746908      and     x8, x8, #0x7ffffff000
    5320: d3557675      ubfx    x21, x19, #21, #9       /* pmd_offset_lockless() */
    ...
    5334: f8757ac8      ldr     x8, [x22, x21, lsl #3]  /* pmdp_get() */

[1] https://lore.kernel.org/all/0019d675-ce3d-4a5c-89ed-f126c45145c9@kernel.org/

Signed-off-by: David Hildenbrand (Arm) <david@kernel.org>

[PATCH RESEND v2 16/21] openrisc/pgtable: drop __pmd_offset()
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>


The macro is unused, and will collide with a folded page table helper.
Let's just remove it.

Signed-off-by: David Hildenbrand (Arm) <david@kernel.org>
---

[PATCH RESEND v2 15/21] x86: mm: carve out the generic
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>

The behaviour of pXdp_get() will change with generic compile-time folded
page tables. To prepare for that, rework effective_prot() to ignore any
folded page tables entries, as it will unconditionally get called by
ptdump core with pXd_val(pXdp_get()), and the value for folded entries
really should be ignored.

For this, introduce mm_first_pgtable_level() to identify the first real
page-table level, move mm_pXd_folded() in !__ASSEMBLER__ and
update effective_prot with higher_prot only when the current entry is not
folded nor the first level.

There should be no functional change.

[PATCH RESEND v2 12/21] mm/pgtable: redefine PGTABLE_LEVEL enum
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>

The PGTABLE_LEVEL enum values appear to be ordered in the opposite direction
from their intended usage.

Intuitively, higher page table levels should have smaller enum values,
but PTE is currently assigned 0.

For consistency and cleanup, reverse the enum value ordering.

Since there is no current user relies on these numbers to be ascending from
PTE->PGD, so it can be changed to be ascending from PGD->PTE, in the
direction of the page table walk.

Reviewed-by: David Hildenbrand (Arm) <david@kernel.org>
Signed-off-by: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>

[PATCH RESEND v2 13/21] x86/mm: Introduce helper for checking
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>


There is an existing variable (direct_gbpages) that says whether the
kernel can and should use 1G pages in the direct map. It is driven
by a bunch of other machinery. At least:

 1. Hardware support for 1G pages
 2. Kconfig support for 1G direct mappings
 3. Kernel command line overrides

Most code just checks the 'direct_gbpages' variable itself.  But there
are cases where 1G mappings are compile-time disabled (via
X86_DIRECT_GBPAGES) and 'direct_gbpages' is always 0. Unfortunately,
that constraint is invisible to the compiler.

This opacity has been historically functionally harmless; it only
leaves a bit of dead code. But, there are plans to tigthen up the
compile-time checks around folded page table levels. The build will
break if that dead code appears reachable to the compiler. Making
the compile-time config visible to the compiler fixes the build.

Add a helper to replace 'direct_gbpages' checks. Check the Kconfig
option and base CPU support before looking at the variable.

This lets the compiler optimize things better, especially
collapse_pud_page() where most of the function can now be optimized
out when the PUD level is folded.

Notes:

Use boot_cpu_has() instead of cpu_feature_enabled(). There's no
required/disabled features for 1G pages themselves
(X86_DIRECT_GBPAGES is for kernel mappings only) and the
static_cpu_has() infrastructure is just gets in the compiler's way.

This makes 64-bit build marginally larger (20 bytes in one compile)
and 32-bit builds less marginally _smaller_ (~700 bytes).

Signed-off-by: Dave Hansen <dave.hansen@linux.intel.com>
Reviewed-by: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>
Tested-by: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>
Link: https://lore.kernel.org/all/20260902-dummy_ptxp3-v3-15-5d8f5b17c25c@arm.com/ [1]
Reviewed-by: Sohil Mehta <sohil.mehta@intel.com>
---

[PATCH RESEND v2 11/21] riscv: mm: use proper set_pXd() for
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>

The behaviour of pXdp_get() will change with generic compile-time folded
page tables, making it invalid to use set_pXd() with the result of
pXdp_get(). Instead, the appropriate first-level set_pXd() must be paired
with the corresponding pXdp_get().

Therefore, call the appropriate set_pXd() for generic compile-time folded
page tables so that vmalloc_fault() continues to handle folded entries
correctly when checking pXd_present().

There should be no functional change.

Since most architectures use a similar pattern to handle vmalloc fault
by syncing the faulted entry, it would be worth cleaning this up by
introducing a common helper in the near future.

[PATCH RESEND v2 07/21] mm/pgtable: define
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>


The default helpers fallback to pud_offset(), passing a pointer to the
stack value.

Let's provide variants that do exactly what (pgd|p4d|pud)_offset() do,
but ignore the passed pgd_t/p4d_t/pud_t, consequently not working on a
pointer to the stack value like the default handlers would.

With this patch, Drop the arm64 custom variant that did that. As it documents:

	Note: reusing the original pointer means that it may be
	dereferenced the same (live) page-table entry multiple times.
	This is safe because it is still only loaded once in the
	context of each level and the CPU guarantees same-address
	read-after-read ordering.

Do not document this, as the double read will be removed in the next patch.

Signed-off-by: David Hildenbrand (Arm) <david@kernel.org>

[PATCH RESEND v2 00/21] mm: change behavior of
Author: Yeoreum Yun <yeoreum.yun@arm.com>

Using ptep_get() and its counterparts in common code is suboptimal on
kernel configurations with generic compile-time folded page tables.
By default, ptep_get() and its friends expands to READ_ONCE(),
forcing the compiler to emit a load even when the value is not used afterwards.

This issue was recently reported by Christophe Leroy [1] for ppc32
preventing futher code conversion to ptep_get()/pmdp_get()/... helper
and the same behavior can also be observed on arm64 when built with
2- or 3-level page tables

e.g) perf_get_page_size() in arm64 with CONFIG_PGTABLE_LEVEL=3:

00000000000052a0 <perf_get_page_size>:
    ...
    52dc: d53b4234     	mrs	x20, DAIF
    52e0: d50343df     	msr	DAIFSet, #0x3
    ...
    52fc: d35e9a69     	ubfx	x9, x19, #30, #9        /* pud_offset_lockless() */
    5300: f9403508     	ldr	x8, [x8, #0x68]
    5304: f869790a     	ldr	x10, [x8, x9, lsl #3]   /* pudp_get() */
    5308: f90007ea     	str	x10, [sp, #0x8]
    530c: f8697908     	ldr	x8, [x8, x9, lsl #3]    /* pudp_get() */
    ...
    5360: 90000009     	adrp	x9, 0x5000 <perf_prepare_sample+0x548>
    5364: 92746908     	and	x8, x8, #0x7ffffff000
    5368: d3557675     	ubfx	x21, x19, #21, #9       /* pmd_offset_lockless() */
    ...
    5394: f8757ac8     	ldr	x8, [x22, x21, lsl #3]  /* pmdp_get() */

Though PGTABLE_LEVEL=3, since the pudp_get() still remain with
READ_ONCE(), there's redundant load for the pud which is folded.

To prevent generating suboptimal code, make pXdp_get() return a dummy
entry for compile-time folded page tables, make the helpers such as
pXd_offset()/pXd_offset_lockless(), set_pXd() validate dummy entries
at compile time to catch the wrong usage and prohibit calls to
pXd_page() in pgtable-nopXd.h.

This series does not change the behaviour of existing code that directly
manipulates folded page-table levels using set_pgd(), pgd_page_vaddr(), and
related helpers. Those helpers continue to behave as before.

The new restrictions only apply to code that adopts the pXdp_get()-based
access model for compile-time folded page tables.

As the pXdp_get() can return *dummy* entry, some of code using
the stack value where saves the pXdp_get() could be a problematic:

  1. Passing address of stack value where saves the pXdp_get() result
     to pXd_offset() for example:

       pud_t *pudp, pud;
       pmd_t *pmdp;

       pud = pudp_get(pudp, address);
       pmdp = pmd_offset(&pud, pud, address);

     (e.g. host_pfn_mapping_level() in loongarch).

  2. Using the pXdp_get() result to use as argument of pXd_val() and
     to check prot without checking pgtable is folded.
     for example, x86's effective_prot().

  3. Using set_pXd() with pXdp_get() will set problematic dummy entry
     in folded page table like:

       set_pXd(pxdp, pXdp_get(pxdp_k));

  4. Using pgd_page_vaddr() to get the first-level pgtable.
     passing dummy pxdp_get() for pgd_page_vaddr() will return wrong
     address. Therefore, make pgd_page_vaddr() and pXd_pgtable() to
     trigger the error for improper usage with folded dummy entry in the
     generic compile-time folded pgtable.

Thanksfully, above cases are rare since (1) most of usage using
pXd_offset() with result of upper pXd_offset(), (2) it's extreamely
rare to use pXd_val() for non-leaf entry in the kernel,
(3) is to handle the vmalloc_fault or set the first level of page table
and (4) to setup early page table and etc.

Therefore, properly handle this uncommon and problematic pattern, and
document the current design of compile-time folded page tables.

This patch is based on mm-unstable.

Future work
===========
 - print_bad_page_map() and show_pte() still prints dummy values
   instead of printing the same content for all generic compile-time
   folded page tables. We might want to skip printing dummy values later.

 - We currently catch abuse of dummy values on the stack at compile-time by
   relying on constant propagation by the compiler. Usama's work [3] on using
   distinct types for sw vs. hw PTEs could help here as well."

 - Clean up vmalloc fault handling by synchronizing the vmalloc entry on
   32-bit architectures. This code is almost identical across architectures.

 - Unfortunately, the current design of compile-time folded page tables appears
   to be internally consistent but confusing. For example,
   when CONFIG_PGTABLE_LEVELS is 2, p4d, pud, and pmd are expected to
   be folded into pgd. However, the architecture code uses set_pmd()
   to update the top-level page-table entry, even though it includes pgtable-nopmd.h.

   In the future, it would be good to eliminate this source of confusion,
   possibly by treating all folded upper levels consistently as dummy wrappers
   around the highest real page-table level:

         NOPGD
   --> +------+           P4D
       | ptr0 |-------> +------+           PUD
       +------+         | ptr0 |-------> +-----+
                        | ptr1 |-        | ptr | -------> ...
                        | ptr2 | \       | ptr |
                        | ptr3 |  \        ...
                          ...      \
                                    \        PUD
                                     +----> +-----+
                                            | ptr | -------> ...
                                            | ptr |
                                              ...

Link: [1] https://lore.kernel.org/all/0019d675-ce3d-4a5c-89ed-f126c45145c9@kernel.org/
Link: [2] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20251113014656.2605447-1-samuel.holland@sifive.com/
Link: [3] https://lore.kernel.org/r/74182e50-b54f-4d2d-a27f-3a59a538d6bc@arm.com

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: Add support for self-hosted debug in protected guests
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

pKVM hides DebugVer from protected guests and makes their debug
registers RAZ/WI or UNDEFINED, so they don't have breakpoints,
watchpoints or single-step. For a non-protected guest, the host sets the
owner of the debug registers at vcpu load, from the guest's MDSCR_EL1
and OS Lock, and EL2 copies the owner and the registers in at each
entry. However, the host can't see a protected guest's registers.

Set the owner at EL2 for a protected vCPU instead, by the rules the host
uses for a non-protected guest, but keep MDCR_EL2.TDA set when the guest
is the owner. A trap handler returns to the guest without going back
through the world switch, so a handler that changes the owner switches
the debug registers itself. The PSCI CPU_ON reset runs after the vcpu
load, so it sets the owner again.

Advertise DebugVer, capped at V8P8 as for a non-protected guest, and the
breakpoint, watchpoint and context-aware breakpoint counts. The other
debug registers that took an UNDEF behave as they do for a non-protected
guest that doesn't own its debug registers.

Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---

Notes:
    Commit 3a81a814437d ("KVM: arm64: Expose self-hosted debug regs as
    RAZ/WI for protected guests") made these registers RAZ/WI and left
    debug support for later.
    
    Tested by running the breakpoint_test_arm64 kselftest in a protected
    guest under kvmtool. It passes on hVHE and nVHE hosts under QEMU,
    and on hVHE again after offlining and onlining the guest CPU it runs
    on. That test has no single-step case, so I checked single-step with
    the pkvm_exceptions test from my pKVM selftests series, coming soon.
    
    Based on Linux 7.4-rc1 (6628e7d4c6b25).

[PATCH v5] KVM: PPC: Book3S HV: Avoid spurious interrupts caused by LPCR_MER bit
Author: Gautam Menghani <gautam@linux.ibm.com>

A huge number of spurious interrupts can be seen immediately after a KVM
on PowerNV guest boots up in XIVE mode.

$ cat /proc/interrupts  | grep SPU
SPU:     223705     192439     273526     147623   Spurious interrupts

This bug was introduced by commit ecd10702baae5 ("KVM: PPC: Book3S HV:
Handle pending exceptions on guest entry with MSR_EE"). The root cause
is that once LPCR_MER bit is set, it is supposed to be reset by
software. But when a vCPU starts running with LPCR_MER set, the vCPU does
not exit back to the host until the decrementer expires or there is an
hcall, etc. This is because KVM on PowerNV guests have support for
native XIVE, so they are not dependent on host for interrupt emulation.
Due to this behaviour, a huge number of spurious interrupts are seen
since LPCR_MER continues to be set and LPCR_MER cannot be reset until the
vCPU exits to the host.

Fix this behaviour by not using the LPCR_MER bit whenever native XIVE is
available (currently in case of KVM on PowerNV only), as the XIVE hardware
can present interrupts to the KVM guest vCPU directly. So the LPCR_MER
functionality is not required. This reduces the number of spurious
interrupts drastically.

Fixes: ecd10702baae5 ("KVM: PPC: Book3S HV: Handle pending exceptions on guest entry with MSR_EE")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org # 6.8+
Reported-by: Timothy Pearson <tpearson@raptorengineering.com>
Closes: https://lore.kernel.org/linuxppc-dev/582904882.11159.1786719390349.JavaMail.zimbra@raptorengineeringinc.com
Signed-off-by: Gautam Menghani <gautam@linux.ibm.com>
---
v5:
1. Drop the redundant check for the presence of the native XIVE
device. (Ritesh)

v4:
1. If interrupt mode is changed to XICS during guest reboot, MER should
be used. (Amit)

v3:
1. Continue the use of LPCR_MER when kernel-irqchip=off (Sashiko)

v2:
1. Handle the case where xive_interrupt_pending() is true and also the
external exception bit is set. (Narayana)

 arch/powerpc/kvm/book3s_hv.c | 2 +-
 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v4] x86/virt/tdx: Skip TDH.SYS.DISABLE if the TDX module is not initialized
Author: Challvy Tee <challvy.tee@gmail.com>

Commit 5b25f249be32 ("x86/tdx: Disable the TDX module during kexec and
kdump") made native_machine_crash_shutdown() call tdx_sys_disable(),
whose only gate is compile time (CONFIG_INTEL_TDX_HOST).  A kernel that
never initialized the TDX module therefore still issues
TDH.SYS.DISABLE.

In VMX non-root operation on a SEAM-capable host, SEAMCALL causes a VM
exit instead of a native #UD.  KVM injects #UD only since
commit 9d7dfb95da2c ("KVM: VMX: Inject #UD if guest tries to execute
SEAMCALL or TDCALL"), first released in v6.18.  On an older host the
VMM gets KVM_EXIT_INTERNAL_ERROR and the vCPU never resumes, so
machine_kexec() is never reached and no vmcore is written.  Kdump is
thus broken only in guests on SEAM-capable hosts whose hypervisor does
not handle the exit; plain kexec is unaffected because tdx_shutdown()
only runs as a syscore op, registered after init_tdx_module() succeeds.

Gate the SEAMCALL on X86_FEATURE_TDX_HOST_PLATFORM before issuing it,
the way paddr_is_tdx_private() already guards its own SEAMCALL in this
file.  Without that feature the module is never initialized, so there
is nothing to disable; on a TDX host the gate is a no-op.

It also decouples kdump from the host KVM version -- a guest cannot
require its host to inject the #UD.

Fixes: 5b25f249be32 ("x86/tdx: Disable the TDX module during kexec and kdump")
Suggested-by: Kiryl Shutsemau <kas@kernel.org>
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Challvy Tee <challvy.tee@gmail.com>
---
v3 -> v4:
 - Gate on X86_FEATURE_TDX_HOST_PLATFORM instead of
   tdx_module_state.initialized, guarding the SEAMCALL itself the way
   paddr_is_tdx_private() already does, as suggested in review.
 - Drop the v3 rationale about covering the syscore and future callers.
 - Retested with this predicate: kdump produces a vmcore on the
   Alibaba ECS Emerald Rapids guest (6.12.0-278.el10 base).
   v3: https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261030154851.907726-1-challvy.tee@gmail.com/

Hypervisor -- KVM on all five (CPUID leaf 0x40000000), across three
clouds: Alibaba Cloud ECS, AWS EC2 and Tencent Cloud CVM.

Host log -- yes, from the Alibaba ECS host of the Emerald Rapids guest:

  KVM internal error. Suberror: 4
  extra data[0]: 4c
  extra data[1]: 2f
  RAX=0000000000000045 RDI=0000000000000045 RIP=ffffffff855422fe
  R12=8000020300000000 R13=8000000300000000 R14=8000020200000000
  Code=... 4c 8b 5e 28 <66> 0f 01 cf 72 05 ...
  check runstate transition: 'running' -> 'internal-error'

Suberror 4 is KVM_INTERNAL_ERROR_UNEXPECTED_EXIT_REASON; the two data
words are the exit reason (0x4c = EXIT_REASON_SEAMCALL) and
last_vmentry_cpu, i.e. kvm_prepare_unexpected_reason_exit().

RAX and RDI hold 0x45 = TDH_SYS_DISABLE and RIP is at the '66 0f 01 cf'
SEAMCALL opcode in __seamcall.  RAX still holds the input leaf rather
than a status code: the SEAMCALL never returned.

Reproduced with 'echo c > /proc/sysrq-trigger'.  All five have
CONFIG_INTEL_TDX_HOST=y, kexec_crash_loaded=1, a kernel changelog
naming both backported commits, and "TDX not supported by the host
platform" in dmesg:

  Microarch          x86_vfm  Cloud          Kernel             echo c
  Sapphire Rapids    0x68F    AWS EC2        6.12.0-278.el10    no vmcore
  Granite Rapids     0x6AD    AWS EC2        6.12.0-278.el10    no vmcore
  Emerald Rapids     0x6CF    Alibaba ECS    6.12.0-278.el10    no vmcore
  Granite Rapids     0x6AD    Alibaba ECS    6.12.0-278.el10    no vmcore
  Granite Rapids     0x6AD    Tencent CVM    6.12.0-278.el10    no vmcore

That dmesg line is printed by tdx_enable() when
X86_FEATURE_TDX_HOST_PLATFORM is clear, so the feature this patch tests
is absent on all five.

Controls, each producing a vmcore: the same platform on a kernel
without 5b25f249be32 (tdx_sys_disable absent from kallsyms), platforms
without SEAM support (Skylake, Cascade Lake, Ice Lake, AMD), and plain
kexec.

With the gate applied kdump produces a vmcore on the Alibaba ECS
Emerald Rapids guest, retested with this predicate.  The Granite
Rapids guest was verified with the v3 predicate and not re-run; the
AWS and Tencent guests are reproduction evidence only.  No TDX host was
available, so the no-op case is reasoned from the code, not measured.

 arch/x86/virt/vmx/tdx/tdx.c | 3 +++
 1 file changed, 3 insertions(+)

[PATCH v3] x86/virt/tdx: Do not print error message on non-present feature
Author: Jiri Slaby (SUSE) <jirislaby@kernel.org>

Before commit 165e77353831 below, the kernel did not print anything
during boot if X86_FEATURE_TDX_HOST_PLATFORM was not present, unless TDX
enablement was explicitly requested via a module parameter.

After the commit, when attempting to load TDX automatically, this is
emitted to the log:
  virt/tdx: TDX not supported by the host platform

It is emitted with the "error" level, hence it is disturbing. It is not
an error to run CPUs without the feature.

Drop this silent-boot-breaking error message for the automatic
initialization path.

Signed-off-by: Jiri Slaby (SUSE) <jirislaby@kernel.org>
Fixes: 165e77353831 ("KVM: x86/tdx: Do VMXON and TDX-Module initialization during subsys init")
Reviewed-by: Kiryl Shutsemau (Meta) <kas@kernel.org>
Reviewed-by: Nikolay Borisov <nik.borisov@suse.com>
Reviewed-by: Sean Christopherson <seanjc@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Rick Edgecombe <rick.p.edgecombe@intel.com>
Cc: Dan Williams <dan.j.williams@intel.com>

[PATCH kvmtool v4 17/23] arm64: Generate GICv5 PCI INTx specifiers
Author: Sascha Bischoff <sascha.bischoff@arm.com>

[PATCH kvmtool v4 00/23] arm64: Support GICv5-based guests
Author: Sascha Bischoff <sascha.bischoff@arm.com>

This series adds support for GICv5-based VMs to kvmtool. These are
exposed as --irqchip=gicv5 and --irqchip=gicv5-its, where the former
only creates a vGICv5 device and the latter adds in a GICv5 ITS. The
GICv5 specification can be found at [1].

The GICv5 support in KVM needed by --irqchip=gicv5 can be found as of
Linux v7.4-rc1. KVM support for GICv5 ITS has been posted at
[2]. Thus, --irqchip=gicv5 works with the corresponding KVM changes,
while --irqchip=gicv5-its cannot yet be used with upstream KVM.

The series teaches kvmtool to create the GICv5 irqchip and ITS KVM
devices. It describes the GICv5 CPU interface, IRS and ITS in the
guest DT, updates the timer and PMU interrupt descriptions, and
generates GICv5 interrupt specifiers for platform devices and PCI. It
also maps PCI MSI requester IDs to the ITS.

Since [3], there have been a lot of changes to the core plumbing
introduced by the GICv5 support. Thanks to Vladimir's suggestions, it
has been somewhat decoupled from the GICv5 support itself, and the
split is now much cleaner. Thanks, Vladimir! The IRS support is built
up and exposed before the ITS support is added, as was the case
previously. This allows, for example, the --irqchip=gicv5 config to be
merged while leaving the ITS changes under review.

Compared with v3 [3]:

* Split GIC device address setup into CPU interface, redistributor and
  distributor helpers. The existing MSI helper now uses the global MSI
  base, and the GICv2 PPI CPU-mask check uses irqchip ordering.

* Split IRQ-offset support into generic infrastructure and separate
  GICv5 configuration.

* Use configured SPI/PPI bases and FDT types in interrupt and DT code,
  with a helper for encoding the PMU's GICv5 PPI INTID. Remove the
  unused GICv5 state.

* Give GICv5 FDT interrupt types distinct names and emit CPU phandles
  for all arm64 guests; the GICv5 IRS references those phandles.

* Sync the UAPI headers with v7.4-rc1.

The v7.4-rc1 header sync includes GICv5 IRS UAPI, but not GICv5 ITS
UAPI. Patch 19 mirrors the ITS definitions from the pending KVM series
[2]. Once that UAPI lands upstream, the headers should be resynced
against the latest Linux headers before merging the ITS support.

Thanks,
Sascha

[1] https://developer.arm.com/documentation/aes0070/latest
[2] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261102194454.4086911-1-sascha.bischoff@arm.com/
[3] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261016153939.913764-1-sascha.bischoff@arm.com/

[PATCH v1] KVM: arm64: Fast path transparent_hugepage_adjust() for
Author: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>

transparent_hugepage_adjust() must walk the userspace stage-1 to detect
if a PMD-level mapping is installed. If it is, then a stage-2 block
mapping can be installed. Even though this stage-1 walk is most likely
cache hot (in most cases, the mapping has just been installed), walking
a page-table is still costly. This walks accounts for ~5% of the
user_mem_abort() execution time on an Apple M2 big core. This makes a
fast path interesting.

While we can't be sure by solely looking at the folio if the stage-1 is
a block mapping as userspace has multiple ways to split it, we however
can infer from its size if the stage-1 mapping is __smaller__ than
PMD_SIZE. Anonymous memory or memfd faults will always allocate a folio
big enough for the mapping. The same applies when collapsing into a
block mapping with khugepaged or MADV_COLLAPSE.

Add a fast path based on the folio size. Whenever it is too small for a
PMD_SIZE mapping, bail out early and do not walk the stage-1. This is
interesting for systems where PMD_SIZE THPs are disabled either at
build, or at runtime. Systems under heavy memory pressure will also
benefit from the fast path.

Signed-off-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c | 42 ++++++++++++++++++++++++------------------
 1 file changed, 24 insertions(+), 18 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v3 23/23] KVM: arm64: Stage-2 huge mappings for protected VMs
Author: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>

Enable PMD-sized stage-2 block mappings for protected VMs. This is
possible whenever the stage-1 mapping allows it, that is, if it is
itself backed by THPs.

When a THP is found, an entire PMD_SIZE mapping is donated to the guest,
unless it overlaps an existing mapping. The new PMD_SIZE mapping can
only be broken down via the HVC __pkvm_host_split_guest() which the
hypervisor can request with PKVM_HYP_REQ_SPLIT.

Signed-off-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c  | 128 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++---------------
 arch/arm64/kvm/pkvm.c |  34 +++++++----
 2 files changed, 104 insertions(+), 58 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v3 22/23] KVM: arm64: Raise PKVM_HYP_REQ_SPLIT on guest to
Author: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>

Pages shared by the guest with the host are annotated into the guest
stage-2. If the shared page is backed by a huge mapping, we need to
split it first.

Raise a PKVM_HYP_REQ_SPLIT for the host on the guest HVC MEM_SHARE, if a
block exists (-E2BIG) and replay the HVC (by rewinding the ELR).

At the moment, the guest HVC MEM_SHARE is single-page only. This means
unsharing is ensured to find a PTE-level mapping. However, as this will
most likely be extended later, raise the same PKVM_HYP_REQ_SPLIT on the
guest HVC MEM_UNSHARE.

Signed-off-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/pkvm.c | 43 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++----
 1 file changed, 38 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

[PATCH v3 21/23] KVM: arm64: Add PKVM_HYP_REQ_SPLIT
Author: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>

With the upcoming support for stage-2 huge mappings for protected VMs,
we need a way to split blocks. Since the host has its own "copy" of the
guest stage-2 in the pkvm_mappings rb-tree, the split must be done
simultaneously for both that tree and the guest stage-2. Therefore the
hypervisor can't do it on its own and must rely on the host for this
operation.

Create a pKVM hypervisor request to ask the host to split a specified
region of the guest. On this request, the host can synchronise the split
of both guest stage-2 (HVC __pkvm_host_split_guest) and the
pkvm_mappings tree. It ensures a concurrent VM teardown can't observe a
PMD_SIZE pkvm_mapping while the guest stage-2 is PAGE_SIZE.

Signed-off-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
---
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_host.h |   5 +
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_pkvm.h |   5 +-
 arch/arm64/kvm/pkvm.c             | 148 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 arch/arm64/kvm/trace_pkvm.h       |   4 +-
 4 files changed, 160 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)
